KEYWORDS: Dasaratha, ministers, Ramayana
jai_balayya_ew
jai_balayya_ew
Dasaratha's eight ministers are Dhrsti, Jayanta, Vijaya, Surastra, Rastravardhana, Akopa, Dharmapala, and Sumantra.  Sumantra is also specifically mentioned as his charioteer.

Vamana, the dwarf incarnation of Vishnu, resided at the hermitage that became known as Siddhagrama.

KEYWORDS: valmiki, students, disciples, followers, learners
jai_balayya_ew
jai_balayya_ew
Kusa, Lava, and Bharadwaja are Valmiki's students.  Kusa and Lava were taught the Ramayana by Valmiki, and Bharadwaja is identified as Valmiki's disciple.

jai_balayya_ew
KEYWORDS: payasam, kaikeyi, eaten, Ramayana
jai_balayya_ew
jai_balayya_ew
Kaikeyi received a portion of the divine payasam (pudding).  Initially, she received half of what remained after Kausalya and Sumitra's portions. However, the texts are slightly ambiguous about the exact amount, with some suggesting Dasaratha then gave the final portion to Sumitra.
jai_balayya_ew
jai_balayya_ew
KEYWORDS: payasam, kausalya, eaten
jai_balayya_ew
jai_balayya_ew
Kausalya received half of the divine payasam (rice pudding) initially.  Later, the remaining portions were divided among Sumitra and Kaikeyi, with Sumitra receiving an additional portion.
jai_balayya_ew
jai_balayya_ew
KEYWORDS: payasam, sumitra, eaten
jai_balayya_ew
jai_balayya_ew
Sumitra received two portions of the divine payasam (rice pudding).  King Dasaratha initially gave her half of the remaining portion after giving half to Kausalya. Then, after giving half of what remained to Kaikeyi, he gave the final portion to Sumitra as well.
jai_balayya_ew
jai_balayya_ew
KEYWORDS: Payasam, Kaikeyi, eaten
jai_balayya_ew
jai_balayya_ew
Kaikeyi received a portion of the divine payasam (pudding).  Initially, she received half of the remaining portion after Kausalya and Sumitra's first shares.  Then, Dasaratha gave the final remaining portion to Sumitra, meaning Kaikeyi did *not* receive the largest share.
jai_balayya_ew
jai_balayya_ew
KEYWORDS: Rama,bow,name
jai_balayya_ew
jai_balayya_ew
The provided texts don't name Rama's bow. They describe Rama breaking *Shiva's bow* in the context of Sita's marriage contest.  While Rama used a bow during his journey with Viswamitra to protect the sage's sacrifices, that bow isn't named.
